# Decisions

The record below describes the SDK, the hosts, the adapters, and measured behavior. Where it conflicts with the conventions above, the conventions apply to new and changed code. Entries written before the namespace API of 2026-10-28 use its old names: `createNanocode` is `App.create` (`nanocode-ts/cloudflare` and `nanocode-ts/vercel`), `aiGateway(ai, gateway, serviceTier, firstByteMs)` is `Gateway.create(ai, { id, serviceTier, firstByteMs })`, `nanocodeHarness` is `Harness.create`, `connectHand`, `prepareHand`, `compileModule` and `handKeeper` are `Hand.connect`, `Hand.prepare`, `Hand.compile` and `Hand.keeper`, `deferMcp` is `Mcp.defer`, `relayHands` is `Bridge.relay`, `openSession` is `Session.open`, `threadEngine` is `Thread.engine` (`src/internal/Thread.ts`), `localEngine` is `AgentRuntime.localEngine`, `new EventHub` is `EventHub.create`, `Timings` is `Timing.create`, `HostFailure` and `RemoteRefusal` are `Contracts.HostFailureError` and `Contracts.RemoteRefusalError`, `DurableObjectSql` is `Sql.create`, `relay`, `relayToken` and `refuseSocket` are `Relay.route`, `Relay.token` and `Relay.refuse`, `cloudflare(...)` and `vercel(...)` are each adapter's `Transport.create`, `openReader` is `Reader.open`, `SqlJournal` and `LocalSql` are `Journal.create` and `LocalSql.create`, `gatewayDispatch` is the Vercel `Gateway.dispatch`, `dialHand`, `attachedHands` and `handRoute` are `Hands.dial`, `Hands.attached` and `Hands.route`, `new Bash`, `new MemoryFileSystem`, `SqlFileSystem(sql, scope)` and `mounted` are `Bash.create`, `MemoryFileSystem.create`, `SqlFileSystem.create` and `Mount.create`, `BASH_COMMANDS` and `DEFAULT_LIMITS` are `Bash.commandNames` and `Bash.defaultLimits`, `preloadPython` is `Python.preload`, `fileResponse`, `uploadFile` and `writeFile` are `Files.response`, `Files.upload` and `Files.write` (`src/internal/Files.ts`), `containerWorkspace` and `vercelSandbox` are `Container.create` and `VercelSandbox.create`, `createMachines`, `createWallets` and `walletClass` are `Machines.create`, `Wallets.create` and `Wallets.subclass`, `createConfig`, `modelItems` and `thinkingItems` are `Config.create`, `Pickers.models` and `Pickers.thinking`, and an uppercase constant is a camelCase member of its module (`MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES` is `Files.maxUploadBytes`).

Durable Values. These values outlive a deploy or cross a wire, so a change to them breaks stored state or peers: `Remote.handAccepted` is `{"type":"ack","ids":[]}` in that key order, as the wire format fixes it; the `nanocode_cell_journal_batches` table (`scope`, `session_id`, `cell_id`, `seq`, `body`, where `body` is one or more of the SDK's `CellJournalUpdate` JSON lines joined by newlines and `seq` is the first line's) and the `nanocode_cell_journals` table it replaced (the same columns with one update per row, still read and cleared), the `nanocode_cell_tools` table (`scope`, `digest`, `body`, where `body` is the JSON array of a cell header's tool catalog and `digest` the first 32 hex characters of its BLAKE3) and the marker `[{"$nanocode_tools": <digest>}]` that stands for that catalog in a journal line's `header.tools` in the session's SQL store and the Cloudflare session's `hand_requests` table (`hand`, `runtime`, `id`, `scope`, `session_id`, `call_id`, `deadline`, `replied`, `reply`, where `reply` is the hand's reply frame text); the Vercel harness's settlement keys (`nanocode.settled:<batches>`), its attachment markers (`<nanocode-attachments>`, which Workflow replays parse out of stored prompts), its subagent command marker (`Harness.subagentText`, which stored steer submissions begin with, followed by the command's JSON), the `nanocode.wallet` document kind at version 1 in `nanocode-ts/machines/vercel`, and the `Symbol.for('nanocode-ts.vercel.hands')` and `Symbol.for('nanocode-ts//vercel//module')` globals, which let every copy of the package in one process share its hands and its compiled module. `src/vercel/Wasm.ts` reads `../nanocode_wasm.wasm` relative to itself, so the module stays one folder below the package root. In `src/bash/Bash.ts` later command tables shadow earlier ones, so the order of their spreads decides which handler a name runs. `nanocode-ts/cloudflare` and `nanocode-ts/machines/cloudflare` also export their Durable Object classes flat, because Wrangler binds Durable Object classes by export name. `@vercel/oidc` is an optional peer, so the Vercel gateway imports it only when no API key is set. The WebAssembly fingerprint hashes `src/wasm` and `scripts/build.mjs` and `scripts/wasm.mjs` byte for byte, so `vite.config.ts` keeps the formatter and linter off them.

nanocode-ts and the hosts built on it (nanocodexyz/durable-cloudflare, nanocodexyz/durable-vercel, nanocodexyz/nanocode-site) send models only upstream nanocode's native prompts and tool text: no system or developer instructions of their own, no workspace or sandbox instructions, no tool description overrides (Georgios, 2026-10-25). What models need goes into nanocodexyz/nanocode (its WASM `exec` defaults defer `mcp__*` tools, brief the rarely used subagent tools and note QuickJS, nanocodexyz/nanocode#1085). `instructions` stays an option for other hosts. Hands mode's placement text (`PLACEMENT_INSTRUCTIONS`, which the SDK asks brains without a local evaluator to add) goes into `environment.project_instructions`, after the workspace's AGENTS.md, not into `instructions`, because the harnesses' instructions replace their system prompt (Codex's `system_prompt`, Claude's `system`): until 2026-11-03 every session in hands mode, which tempoxyz/gateway's agents always are, sent that one paragraph as its whole system prompt, 592 characters on Claude instead of Claude Code's 5,351, and 496 on Codex instead of its 18,274. `node bench/probe.ts <wasm> claude|codex` opens a hands session with a model that answers 400 and prints the first request's system prompt and where the placement text landed; with this change it lands in Claude's system after the upstream blocks, and in Codex's first user message as `# AGENTS.md instructions for /workspace`, beside `<environment_context>`. Mentions below of `LOCAL_INSTRUCTIONS`, `BASH_CODE_MODE_INSTRUCTIONS`, `BASH_REMOTE_PYTHON_INSTRUCTIONS`, the sandbox instructions, `briefTools`, Codex's file-only workspace default and `deferMcp`'s default deferral describe what was removed then: `deferMcp` now defers only with `deferred: true`, and Codex gets all of its native workspace tools. With every host instruction removed, a local GLM-5.3 sweep through the Cloudflare demo Worker passed 12 of 12; the chart prompt needed 17 model calls, since nothing tells models to embed a saved image. Second pass (Georgios, 2026-10-25): nanocode-ts defines no tool text that duplicates or rewrites an upstream tool. `deferMcp` only caches MCP `initialize` and `tools/list` (its `describe_tools`/`call_tool` rewriting, `deferred` and `core` are gone; the SDK defers `mcp__*` tools natively), `Bash` has no `tool()`/`bashTool`/`BASH_TOOL_DESCRIPTION` (no host used them), and the machines cloud tools drop `list_files`, `read_file`, `write_file`, `edit_file` and `delete_file` (upstream Read/Edit/Write and `exec_command` cover the session workspace), leaving `http`, `list_secrets` and `wallet` with one factual line each. On WASM, `AgentRuntime.open` reads the workspace's own `AGENTS.override.md` or `AGENTS.md` through the workspace host and passes it as `environment.project_instructions`, as native nanocode loads a workspace's AGENTS.md (nanocodexyz/nanocode's WASM context source only takes it from the host); nanocodexyz/nanocode-site mounts its docs index there. Codex sessions on WASM get every native Codex tool except native-only host routing (`hands`, `runners`): `view_image` reads workspace images through `HostFiles::read_bytes` (the `cell_read` op), and `image_gen__imagegen` posts to `<codex endpoint>/images/generations|edits` through the session's model transport and saves to `generated_images/` through `write_bytes` (`cell_write`), unless the host defines its own `image_gen__imagegen` (nanocodexyz/nanocode#1091). The Cloudflare gateway runs them as the AI Gateway catalog model `openai/<model>` (`gpt-image-2`) and returns OpenAI's `{data: [{b64_json}]}` from the image URL the catalog answers, with edit images as data-URL strings in `images`: AI Gateway added no stored OpenAI key to `openai` provider `images/generations` (401 Missing bearer, while `responses` passed on the same gateway, 2026-10-26). `bench/targets/rust.ts` reads `nc`'s OpenTelemetry traces from a temporary `--home`, which carry Codex token counts, `code_mode.cell` spans and per-session `agent.depth` for subagents.

`bench/` is the optimization and eval harness, in TypeScript with raw-text reports. `node bench/run.ts --targets=cloudflare,vercel,rust --models=haiku,sol,glm [--probes=a,b] [--conc=1] [--label=x]` runs the probes in `bench/probes.ts` (cells, bash, file edits, git clone, fetch, parallel calls, python, WebAssembly, notify, yield and wait, store and load, ALL_TOOLS, web search, subagents, charts) against the live Cloudflare demo (`CF_BASE`, `CF_TOKEN`), the Vercel demo (`VERCEL_BASE`) and native Rust (`nc exec` on a machine with Codex and Claude logins; `nc` has no Workers AI route, so no GLM), grades each answer, and writes `output/bench/<label>.jsonl` and a `.txt` table of pass rate, wall time, model calls, first-call and per-turn input tokens, cached share, and wall time split into model, cells, cold runtime start and host overhead, plus a pass grid. `node bench/overhead.ts` measures Code Mode's own cost in this module in Node with a mock model that answers at once, and `node bench/overhead-native.ts` the same cells in the SDK's native `code_mode` example (`CODE_MODE_BIN`, a release build). `node bench/hands.ts --hand=local|ts|native|docker [--provider=claude|codex] [--cells=a,b] [--n=5]` runs 28 cells, from an empty cell to 500 sequential callbacks, 6 MiB of `text()`, 100,000 `console.log` lines, 10,000 timers, a 10^7 loop and a 200 KB source, through this module's brain in Node (in-memory SQLite, a scripted model that answers at once, a fresh session per cell; each row adds the brain's CPU per turn from `process.cpuUsage()` and the hand process's from `ps`, without the shells it starts, which stay meaningful on a loaded machine where turn times do not) with the cells run in the brain as the Cloudflare and Vercel demos run them (`local`), on a TypeScript hand (`ts`, `bench/hand-ts.ts`) or on a native `nanocode hand` (`native`, `NANOCODE_BIN`; `docker` runs the Linux arm64 binary in `LINUX_BIN_DIR` in a `rust:1.97-bullseye` container with host networking) over a loopback WebSocket, and writes `output/bench/hands-<label>.txt` with turn and cell medians, socket frames and bytes each way, SQL transactions, statements and bytes, and model request bytes per turn (`HIST=1` lists statements and bytes by kind, `TOP=1` the largest logged events). `node bench/long.ts --hand=local --turns=300` runs one session for that many turns and reports turn time, SQL and request bytes per window and the cost of reopening it. First run, 2026-10-25 (nanocode-ts 08a1208 demos, nc 0.6.7): Cloudflare and Vercel 48 of 48 each, native 32 of 32 (the grader could not see subagents in four native answers, which were correct); host overhead was 9-19% of wall time on Cloudflare, 21-34% on Vercel and 35% on native Haiku, where each run starts a process; an empty cell took 13 ms in Node and 2.2 ms natively, and a million-iteration loop 84 ms and 36 ms in QuickJS. Native logs carry no Codex token counts and no Claude cell spans, and Workers freeze timers between I/O, so those columns read 0 there. The full demo matrix is 13 setups, every model the demos offer: `--targets=cloudflare --models=haiku,sonnet,luna,sol,glm` and `--targets=vercel --models=haiku,sonnet,cglm,ckimi,luna,sol,glm,kimi --conc=2` (`cglm` and `ckimi` are Claude-harness `zai/glm-5.3` and `moonshotai/kimi-k3`, `kimi` Codex `kimi-k3`), 20 probes each with `bigout`, `recursion`, `curl` and the Codex-only `image`; the Cloudflare driver uses nanocode.xyz/cloudflare's public route with a visitor header and both drivers retry 429s. Second run, 2026-10-26 (nanocode-ts 80e7d2d demos): Cloudflare 98 of 98, Vercel 153 of 156, and the three misses (a 524 and a stalled turn on Vercel Claude-harness GLM, a client fetch error) passed on rerun; a full Vercel run costs about $10 of AI Gateway credit.

This repository is nanocode-ts (nanocodexyz/nanocode-ts, formerly nanocode-wasm): the nanocode Rust SDK compiled into one WebAssembly module, the TypeScript host that runs it in any JavaScript runtime that can instantiate WebAssembly, such as Cloudflare Workers, Node, Deno and browsers, and TypeScript adapters for Vercel and Cloudflare, so a host repository writes only TypeScript and builds no Rust. Its sibling `../nanocodex` supplies the SDK; `sdk-revision.json` records the SDK master revision this package builds against; check out that revision in the sibling when preparing a fresh workspace. `.github/workflows/release.yml` builds every push to main against the SDK at `sdk-revision.json`, checked out beside it as `nanocodex/`, with the Rust release `rust-toolchain.toml` pins, checks it, packs it and publishes the tarball as the GitHub release `build-<short commit>`. Before that it publishes the WASM build outputs, `dist/nanocode_wasm.wasm`, `dist/interpreter.wasm`, `dist/models.js` and `dist/models.d.ts` with their digests in `dist/nanocode-wasm.json`, as the asset `nanocode-wasm-<fingerprint>.tgz` of the release `wasm-<fingerprint>` (`node scripts/publish-wasm.mjs --upload`), and checks out the SDK and runs Cargo only when that release does not exist yet. `node scripts/wasm.mjs` prints the fingerprint: the first 20 hex digits of a SHA-256 over `sdk-revision.json`, `Cargo.toml`, `Cargo.lock`, `rust-toolchain.toml`, `scripts/build.mjs`, `scripts/wasm.mjs`, the JavaScript files under `src/wasm`, every `src` Rust file and the interpreter's `Cargo.toml`, `Cargo.lock` and Rust files, so a push that changes only TypeScript reuses the last WASM release. `node scripts/publish-wasm.mjs` without `--upload` writes the asset to `output/`, from `dist/` when its manifest names the current fingerprint, or from a directory or a `build-<commit>` tarball given as its argument, which must come from a commit with the same Rust inputs; only builds against the SDK at `sdk-revision.json` may be uploaded, since the fingerprint does not cover `../nanocodex`. Hosts install the tarball, `"nanocode-ts": "https://github.com/nanocodexyz/nanocode-ts/releases/download/build-<short commit>/nanocode-ts-<short commit>.tgz"`, which their lockfile pins with its integrity: nanocodexyz/durable-cloudflare (formerly nanocode-worker) and nanocodexyz/durable-vercel; nanocodexyz/nanocode-site builds the package from a checkout instead. `dist/` is build output, ignored by Git and listed in `package.json`'s `files`, so the tarball carries the compiled JavaScript, declarations and module and installing it needs neither Rust nor a TypeScript build. To consume a change without waiting for its release, a host pins the commit, `"nanocode-ts": "github:nanocodexyz/nanocode-ts#<commit>"` (`npm install github:nanocodexyz/nanocode-ts#<commit>`, which also updates the lockfile; editing only `package.json` leaves an installed git dependency in place). npm clones the commit, installs its devDependencies and runs `prepare`, `scripts/prepare.mjs`: it keeps `dist/`'s WASM outputs when `dist/nanocode-wasm.json` names the commit's fingerprint and their digests match, else downloads `wasm-<fingerprint>` over HTTPS without authentication (`NANOCODE_TS_WASM_URL` replaces the URL with another URL, a `file:` URL or a path), checks its manifest and copies it into `dist/`, and when no such release exists builds the WASM with `scripts/build.mjs` if Cargo and `../nanocodex` are present, or fails naming the fingerprint. It then runs `npm run build:ts` (both `tsc` builds and `scripts/zyzz.mjs`) unless `output/typescript-build-fingerprint` matches the TypeScript inputs, so npm's second `prepare` while packing the clone is free. Installing a commit that changes only TypeScript takes about 20 seconds; a commit that changes Rust inputs installs once its push to main has published `wasm-<fingerprint>`, and every later TypeScript-only commit at once. `npm install /path/to/nanocode-ts`, its `--install-links` form and `npm pack` run the same `prepare`, as does `npm install` in a checkout. pnpm runs a git dependency's `prepare` only when the host's `pnpm-workspace.yaml` lists it under `allowBuilds` (`nanocode-ts@<resolved spec>: true`). Commits before the releases kept `dist/` in Git, so their `github:nanocodexyz/nanocode-ts#<commit>` pins still install. Generic agent, durability, turn and event logic belongs in the SDK, host-agnostic bridge glue in `src/*.ts`, a platform's runtime and wiring in its adapter under `src/<platform>/` (the whole Durable Object runtime in `src/cloudflare/`, the durable-harness app in `src/vercel/`, each with a browser `transport.ts`), so a host repository only configures and skins it, the store and React chat the hosts share in `src/client/` and `src/react/`, and an application's own choices (its tools, models, authorization policy, pages and commands) in the host repository.

Do not add Cargo features or wasm-bindgen glue. The host-agnostic sources use no platform types or bindings: no `DurableObjectState`, no `@cloudflare/workers-types`, no Node APIs; `tsconfig.json` checks `src/*.ts`, `src/cloudflare/Sql.ts` and both `transport.ts` files against the standard web platform types only, `tsconfig.vercel.json` checks `src/vercel/*.ts` against Node's, and `tsconfig.cloudflare.json` checks `src/cloudflare/*.ts` against `@cloudflare/workers-types` (the runtime imports `DurableObject` from `cloudflare:workers` and the module as `nanocode-ts/nanocode_wasm.wasm`, which the package resolves to itself); `tsconfig.cloudflare.build.json` emits that directory, which `tsconfig.build.json` leaves out. `package.json` marks only `*.css` as side effects, and every runtime is its own subpath, so a page that imports `nanocode-ts/cloudflare/transport` or `nanocode-ts/vercel/transport` bundles none of the Worker, Node or WebAssembly code. Hosts supply everything platform-specific through the interfaces below or take an adapter's. The Rust side is single-threaded and keeps its state in `thread_local` cells, with no `Mutex` or `RwLock`. TypeScript uses only erasable syntax (`erasableSyntaxOnly`, `verbatimModuleSyntax`) and imports with `.ts` extensions, so Node 22.18 or newer runs the sources directly with type stripping from a checkout or a `file:` link, and bundlers such as esbuild and Wrangler compile them. Limits come from host policy passed in, not from constants here.

`package.json` exports the compiled host as `nanocode-ts`, the adapters as `nanocode-ts/vercel` and `nanocode-ts/cloudflare`, the browser client as `nanocode-ts/client`, the website feedback widget and its server handler as `nanocode-ts/feedback` and `nanocode-ts/feedback/server`, the headless React components as `nanocode-ts/react` (their skins, themes and page chrome are nanocodexyz/nds's since 2026-11-09), the code highlighter as `nanocode-ts/highlight`, the model catalog as `nanocode-ts/models`, the simulated shell as `nanocode-ts/bash`, machine payments as `nanocode-ts/machines`, `nanocode-ts/machines/local`, `nanocode-ts/machines/server`, `nanocode-ts/machines/react`, `nanocode-ts/machines/cloudflare` and `nanocode-ts/machines/vercel`, the compiled module as `nanocode-ts/nanocode_wasm.wasm`, with declarations beside each file. `nanocode-ts/models` is generated by `scripts/build.mjs`, which instantiates the module it built and writes `AgentRuntime.modelCatalog()`: each harness's models as the SDK's `ModelInfo` lists them from its `ModelCapabilities`, with their display name, the reasoning efforts allowed and the default, whether it takes the priority service tier (`ModelInfo::fast`), the service tiers (`standard`, `fast`, `ultrafast`) and reasoning modes (`standard`, `pro`) its `ModelCapabilities` list as `service_tiers` and `reasoning_modes`, and the gateways that serve it (`direct`, `vercel`, `workers_ai`), so hosts render pickers and validate requests from the SDK instead of copying its tables; a Claude-harness model outside the catalog, such as `zai/glm-5.3`, stays host policy. `nanocode-ts/client` imports nothing, so a page can load `dist/client.js` as a static module: `liveEvent` maps logged events to the vocabulary the Vercel adapter streams (`text`, `reasoning`, `tool_input`, `tool_start`, `tool_end`, `turn`, `retrying`), `Transcript` reveals text and reasoning at 1 − e^(−t/`revealMs`) per frame (instantly with `revealMs: 0` or reduced motion), posts finished paragraphs (`paragraphs`, which keeps code fences whole) as entries, and folds every tool call into one `ToolCell` entry, as the Rust TUI's `bin/nanocode/src/events.rs` does: `tool_input` deltas stream the call's input into it as `ToolInput` decodes it, `tool_start` replaces it with the call's arguments (`describeTool`: a Code Mode `exec` cell's source without its `// @exec:` pragma as `js`, a shell command, a patch or edit as a diff, a file path as the title), nested calls (`parent_call_id`, or a `<parent>/<n>` call id) become its children, `tool.notification` (`tool_output`) appends to a running cell's output (each notification carries only the new output; the cell keeps its last 64 KiB), and `tool_end` (`finishTool`) sets its status, duration, exit code and output with the `Script …`/`Wall time` header stripped (a `duration_ns` of 0 leaves the duration out: a Worker's clock advances only on I/O, so on 2026-10-23 every Code Mode and `run` cell that only computed and called the SQL-backed `bash` over the Cloudflare transport logged 0 and showed `· 0 ms`, with its `tool.call` and `tool.result` at the same `created_at`); a cell that yields (`Script running with cell ID n`) ends as `yielded`, its posted entry marks its still-running nested calls `yielded` too, and the `wait` call on that cell then owns those calls (they move under it and finish there) and its later nested calls and notifications; before 2026-10-24 those calls stayed `running` in the posted cell and showed a spinner after the turn ended. When a turn ends (its `turn` event, or a send or resume that ends without one), the store marks every cell of that turn still `yielded` or `running` in its posted entries, nested calls included, `completed` for a completed turn and `cancelled` otherwise, and forgets the turn's yielded-cell links, so a Stop never leaves a ↻ or spinner behind. Tool output loses its ANSI escape sequences. A cell is posted once it and every cell started before it finished, and the turn's `turn` event settles any left; a later event for a call that was settled that way, or for a new nested call of such a cell that no `wait` owns, is dropped (the last 1,024 settled call IDs are kept), because on 2026-10-24 after Stop on nanocode.xyz/cloudflare/claude the cancelled cell's nested `bash` call ended 20 s later and showed up as a failed row under the next turn's answer; until then `live(entries)` receives the running cells and then the paragraph being written. Retry, error, steer and failed-turn events become entries (`describe`) and every other event goes to the host's `apply` in order; since 2026-10-27 such an event (one that is not text, reasoning, a tool event or `turn` and adds no entry, such as `usage`) reaches `apply` without ending the paragraph being written, because the Vercel stream sends a model call's `usage` before the last delta of its text, and the paragraph broke there mid-word ("Your plot is at /work" and "space/largest_files.png" as two paragraphs on nanocode.xyz/vercel on 2026-10-27). `liveEvent` carries the logged event's `created_at` as `at`, and entries keep `started_at` and `ended_at` (ms) from it, or from the page's clock for events without it: a paragraph or reasoning summary from its stream's first and latest delta, a cell from its start and duration, a note from when it was sent, so a page shows "Thought for 4s" from saved entries. `turns(entries, live, pending)` groups a transcript into turns (`id`, the user `prompt` entry, `blocks` with consecutive paragraphs and consecutive reasoning merged, each with `live`, the turn's `live`, `started_at` and, once it is over, `ended_at`), and `activity(live, pending)` names what a running turn does (`idle`, `waiting`, `thinking`, `writing` or `running` with the `tool`), `serverEvents` reads a Server-Sent Events body, and `modelItems` and `thinkingItems` build the composer's pickers. `ToolInput` decodes a tool call's input as `tool_input` deltas arrive, in one pass over each delta: `fields` holds each top-level string field of a JSON object, unescaped and growing as the model writes it, and `text()` the only field, `key: value` lines for several, or the raw text of a freeform input such as Codex's `exec` source, so a page can show a file's content or a page's markup while the model writes the call; 600 random chunkings of three inputs (escapes, \u sequences, emoji, a 5 KB field, nested objects and arrays) decoded to the fields `JSON.parse` reads. The three chats, nanocodexyz/durable-vercel's, nanocodexyz/durable-cloudflare's and nanocode.xyz's, share it. `createConfig({ transport, persist, apply })` in `nanocode-ts/client` is the framework-free store the chats share, as wagmi's `createConfig` is: it runs a `Transcript`, keeps `entries`, `live`, `status` and `error` behind `getState` and `subscribe` (`getServerState` is the state a server render without storage produces, which `nanocode-ts/react` hydrates from, so a Next page that renders the chat on the server does not fail hydration with React error 418 when the visitor has a saved conversation), and its `send` notes the prompt and calls the host's `transport.send({ prompt, push, note })`, or `transport.steer` while a turn is pending, settling to idle only after the transcript showed every queued event; a steer's entry (`steer: true`) goes through `Transcript.aside`, which holds it while a paragraph streams and posts it after that paragraph, so a steer does not cut the paragraph in two; `cancel()` calls `transport.cancel` for the running turn, and `cancellable` says whether the transport has one; `resume` runs `transport.resume` for a turn that kept running while the page was closed or the conversation was not open. Each `send` and `resume` is a generation whose parameters carry an `AbortSignal` (`signal`); the store drops the pushes and notes of a generation that is no longer current, and its end no longer touches `status`. With `history: { key, storage, limit }` the store keeps a conversation history instead of one transcript: `conversations` (id, the first prompt as title, last update) and the open `conversation` in its state, each conversation's entries and its transport's `snapshot()` under `<key>:entries:<id>` and `<key>:transport:<id>` in `localStorage` (or `storage`), the index under `<key>:history`, and, while a turn runs, the transcript's running cells, the paragraph being written and its yielded cells (`Transcript.state()`, each text clipped to 64 KiB) under `<key>:live:<id>`. The entries, the live state and the snapshot are written together, in one synchronous step, at most every 250 ms and at once after an entry is posted (a posted entry waits in memory until then; when `localStorage` refuses the live state the store evicts other conversations and, failing that, drops the live state but still saves the cursor), with the snapshot taken when the last processed event arrived, so a reload or a second tab restores the running cells and the paragraph (`Transcript.restore`) and resumes from an event cursor that matches them: before 2026-10-24 the cursor was saved as each event arrived and the live state not at all, so a reload mid-cell lost the running cell, its source and the paragraph being written until the cell ended. Until the same day entries were written as they were posted and the cursor only with the live state, so a refused or skipped live write left the cursor behind the entries and a reload replayed events already shown (once on nanocode.xyz/vercel, a duplicated run row under the final answer). Resume is also idempotent on its own: `Transcript` drops an event whose `seq` it already applied with the same type, call or item and length (the last 4,096 in memory, the last 256 saved with the live state and kept after the turn ends), and the live state keeps the last settled call IDs, so a replay from an old cursor changes nothing, while a takeover drive's new events that reuse a lost `seq` still show. `nanocode-ts/vercel/transport` does not reconnect from a cursor that already carries `;end`. Tabs that share a history coordinate through Web Locks and a `BroadcastChannel` named `nanocode:<key>` (when the page has both): the tab that runs a turn holds the lock `<key>:turn:<conversation>` until its transcript has drained and its last write is saved, and posts its entries, live entries and status for that conversation; another tab showing the conversation follows instead of driving it, shows exactly those entries, writes nothing for it, and forwards its own messages (steers) and Stop to the owner, so steering and cancel stay with one tab. When the lock frees (the turn ended or the owner tab closed), the follower rereads the conversation from storage and calls `resume`, so it continues a turn the closed tab left running. A tab claims the lock before `send` or `resume` and, when another tab changed the conversation's keys since (`storage` events), first rereads its entries, live state and transport snapshot, and it never writes a transport snapshot or live state it did not produce; a tab whose fresh, empty conversation was never saved opens the conversation another tab saves as current. Before 2026-10-25 each tab read storage only at load: on nanocode.xyz/cloudflare/chatgpt and /vercel/chatgpt a second tab showed nothing of a turn sent from the first until reloaded, its own message then started a second turn on the session from its stale cursor, and its next write replaced the first tab's entries, so after a reload that turn was missing from the history. A posted cell keeps at most 64 KiB of input and output, and when `localStorage` refuses a write the store forgets the oldest other conversations until it fits (on 2026-10-24 one 10 MB cell output on the Cloudflare demo stored 1 MB, and every nanocode.xyz demo shares one origin's 5 MB). A send or resume that ends without settling its cells settles them as `cancelled`; `select(id)` reopens one with `transport.restore(snapshot)`, `create()` starts one (and `clear()` does too, keeping the old one listed), `remove(id)` forgets one. While a turn runs they act only when the transport has `resume` (`detachable`): the turn is detached rather than refused, its signal aborts so the transport stops reading and leaves the turn running on the server, the entries it already posted stay in its own conversation (its transcript keeps the paragraph and cells it was showing, in memory, and goes on posting what it had received into that conversation), and `select` then calls `resume`, so reopening the conversation continues the turn from the snapshot's cursor; `remove` of the conversation whose turn runs also calls `cancel`. Without `resume` (an in-tab agent) they still refuse while a turn runs, except `remove` of another conversation. Both transports implement `snapshot` and `restore` with their session state (session id, event cursor, open turn), and honor `signal`: an aborted turn or resume stops its stream and reconnects, keeps the open turn in the snapshot taken when it detached, and never writes to the session state `restore` put in its place. A reconnect that gets 408, 429 or 5xx, or no response, waits `Retry-After` or a delay that doubles from `reconnectMs` up to 15 s and tries again, so a page past the demos' 30 requests a minute goes on following the turn instead of ending it with an error entry. `nanocode-ts/react` (`src/react/index.tsx`, React an optional peer dependency) binds it with wagmi-style hooks: `NanocodeProvider({ config, highlight, skin })`, `useConfig`, `useTranscript` (`entries`, `live`), `useSend` (`send`, `cancel`, `canCancel`, `isPending`, `isIdle`, `error`), `useTurns` (`turns` of the transcript), `useActivity` (`activity`, which `Chat`'s typing line reads), `useNote`, `useHighlighter` and `useConversations` (`conversations`, `current`, `enabled`, `canCreate` and `canSelect`, false while a turn runs unless the config is `detachable`, and `canCreate` also for an empty conversation, `select`, `create`, `remove`), and exports `Chat`, the chat the hosted demos share (the log with `you`/`agent` speakers, the typing line, an `extras` slot, command completion and a host `picker`, the prompt with history on ↑/↓, suggestions (a plain suggestion stays enabled while a turn runs and steers it) and the status line with an `actions` slot, a `stop` button while a cancellable turn runs and, when the config keeps a history, a `history (n)` button that opens a filterable picker of the conversations with `+ New conversation` first), with `EntryView`, `ToolCell`, `Markdown`, `Image` and `Code` for hosts with their own layout. `Markdown` (also exported as `Reply`) renders an assistant entry's markdown with unified (remark-parse with the GFM syntax of micromark-extension-gfm and mdast-util-gfm, remark-rehype, rehype-sanitize, rehype-harden and hast-util-to-jsx-runtime, each a dependency): unless `live` is `false`, `Markdown.complete` from `nanocode-ts/client` closes unfinished bold, italics, strikethrough, inline code, links and table headers while text streams (it hides a dangling `*`, `~~` or backtick and a table row not yet ended by a newline, where streamdown showed them for a moment), `Markdown.blocks` splits the text into memoized blocks so finished blocks do not re-render, and `Markdown.fenced` marks a code block whose fence is still open in the last block. Every element is a headless part, `markdown` (its root, `display: contents` in the nanocode skin), `heading` (`level`), `paragraph`, `list` (`ordered`), `item`, `quote`, `table`, `cell` (`header`, `align`), `rule`, `strong`, `em`, `strike`, `inline`, `link` (only `http(s)` and `mailto`), `figure` and `image`, and fenced code goes to `Code` (with `incomplete` while its fence is open) or the host's `code(language, code)`. Raw HTML in the markdown is not rendered: a remark step turns every `html` node into text, so `<details>`, `<sub>`, `<img>` and `<script>` show as the characters the model wrote and load or run nothing, except the void break tag (`<br>`, `<br/>`, `<br />`, any case), which becomes a line break because models write it inside table cells and list items. The tree is then sanitized (rehype-sanitize with `data:`, `workspace:` and `sandbox:` image sources and the `nanocode:` placeholder of an unfinished link allowed) and hardened (rehype-harden), and newline-only text between block elements is dropped, because entries keep `white-space: pre-wrap`. Until 2026-11-07 the markdown went through streamdown 2.7.1, whose rehype-raw parsed raw HTML with parse5 and kept what rehype-sanitize allowed (`<details>`, `<sub>`, `<kbd>`, and `<img>`, which loaded its URL); streamdown imports rehype-raw statically and puts it in its default plugins, so passing other rehype plugins left parse5 in every host's bundle, and dropping the HTML meant dropping streamdown (`Streamed.tsx` used only its `Streamdown` component, `useIsCodeFenceIncomplete` and default plugins). Checked on 2026-11-07 against tempoxyz/gateway's web build (Vite+, `web/` at 7762bfc) with the release build-84af8f7 and with its package rebuilt from this change: the lazy `Streamed` chunk went from 516.05 KB (158.49 KB gzipped) to 154.28 KB (47.14 KB gzipped), and its source map from 231 sources (124 KB of parse5, 83 KB of streamdown, 47 KB of marked, 27 KB of tailwind-merge, 20 KB of entities, 16 KB of remend) to 156 with none of those; rendering every prefix of two agent replies (612 and 439 characters) with the old and the new `Streamed` through `react-dom/server` threw nowhere and differed only where the new one hides a dangling marker, an unfinished table row or a bracket awaiting its link, finished replies rendered the same elements and parts but for streamdown's Tailwind classes and `data-streamdown` attributes on the root, `thead`, `tbody` and `tr`, an open fence still set `incomplete` on `Code`, and a full render took 1.36 ms against 1.75 ms. In headless Chromium on the gateway's `/agents` against a stub API, a reply with tables, `<br>` in cells and list items, code, links, lists, a quote, headings and inline HTML rendered the same table, 5 line breaks, code block, links, lists and quote before and after; before, `<sub>`, `<kbd>` and `<details>` were elements and `<img src="https://html-img.invalid/…">` became an image link that the page requested, and after they show as text and the page requested no URL but the markdown image's; `<script>`, `<iframe>` and `javascript:` links ran nothing in either. tempoxyz/gateway's site pinned build-165ee8e and deployed it the same day (958e25d, version be7f733a): production served the chunk at 46,526 bytes gzipped, and its home replay, `/demo` (which renders replies with the site's own code) and `/agents` matched the local build pixel for pixel at 1280 and 390 px. nanocodexyz/durable-cloudflare and nanocodexyz/durable-vercel render replies through `Markdown` (`Chat`, `ChatGpt`, `Claude`) and take this when they move past build-84af8f7; gakonst/swarms renders its transcripts with its own react-markdown and does not import `nanocode-ts/react`. `Image` (exported) shows only `http(s)` and `data:image/(png|jpeg|gif|webp|avif|bmp)` URLs (`imageSource`; SVG is left out because a click opens a data image as a same-origin blob), lazily, with its alt text, inside a `figure` link that opens it in a new tab (a data image as a blob URL), falls back to its alt text when it fails to load, and gets `max-width: 100%` inline only when the skin does not style `image`. `finishTool` also collects a tool result's images (`imagesOf`: `input_image` items with `image_url`, `image_url` parts, MCP `image` blocks with `data` and `mimeType`, Anthropic `image` blocks with a base64 or URL `source`, nested in `content`, `content_blocks` or `output`) into the cell's optional `images`, and `ToolCell` shows them under its output as `images`. `ToolCell` renders a cell as the TUI does: a status icon (a spinner and running clock while it runs, then ✓, ✗, ⊘ or ↻), its name, title, duration and details, the input under a │ gutter, nested calls indented under it, and the output, each clipped to a few lines (the input's last lines while it streams) until its header is clicked; with `collapsed` it shows only its header until then. `highlight` is a loader, such as `() => import("nanocode-ts/highlight").then(module => module.highlight)`, and `nanocode-ts/highlight` tokenizes with `@pierre/highlights`, an optional peer dependency, in the nanocode light and dark themes. `NanocodeProvider` calls `highlight` only once a code block with a language other than `text` renders (`useHighlighter(wanted)`), and `Markdown` renders through `React.lazy` from `src/react/Streamed.tsx`, which holds every markdown import: until that chunk loads, an entry shows its text as one `paragraph` inside `markdown`. The chunk loads when the first `Markdown` renders, or when `useSend`'s `send` runs, which starts `preloadMarkdown` because a reply follows. Until 2026-11-07 the provider also started it on the first idle callback after every mount, so tempoxyz/gateway's home page, whose replay shows no assistant entry until it runs, downloaded the markdown chunk (161 KB gzipped then, with streamdown, parse5, micromark, tailwind-merge and marked) on every visit. On 2026-10-24 this took the Cloudflare demo's statically imported JavaScript from 877 KB to 343 KB minified (streamdown, parse5, marked, entities, micromark and tailwind-merge moved to the lazy chunk) and the highlighter's 264 KB off the page load. The components are headless: `src/react/index.tsx` imports no styles, and every element it renders carries `data-nc="<part>"` (`chat`, `log`, `turn`, `speaker`, `entry`, `tool`, `header`, `icon`, `body`, `input`, `send`, `picker`, `status` and the rest of the `Part` union) and its state as `data-*` attributes (`data-kind="assistant"`, `data-status="running"`, `data-live="true"`), so plain CSS can style them. A `Skin` passed to `NanocodeProvider` maps parts to props, `{ parts: { tool: state => ({ className }) }, icons: { running: ["…"] } }`, which `usePart` merges into each element, so a host gives the components its own classes and glyphs without forking them; `useExpanded` (the open tool cells, kept by the provider so `ToolCell` expands outside `Chat` too), `useSkin` and `usePart` serve hosts that compose their own layout from `Markdown`, `ToolCell` and `Code`. The looks are nanocodexyz/nds's (2026-11-09, below): the nanocode skin the hosted demos pass, `<NanocodeProvider skin={skin}>` with `skin` from `nds/skin`, the themes, the clones, `Agent` and the page chrome the demos share, all Zyzz (wevm/zyzz) over nanocode.xyz's tokens. Until then they were `nanocode-ts/react/skin`, `/react/theme`, `/react/themes`, `/react/site`, `/react/clones` and `/react/agent` here, with `scripts/zyzz.mjs` compiling `dist/react` into `nanocode-ts/react/zyzz.css` and a contract beside each module. `src/react/index.tsx` starts with `"use client"` so Next server components can render `site` components; `Code` loads `nanocode-ts/highlight` itself when no `NanocodeProvider` supplies a highlighter. Node's type stripping does not run `.tsx`, so `src/react` is for bundlers only. A bundler that loads `.wasm` imports as `WebAssembly.Module`, as Wrangler does, imports it directly; other hosts compile `dist/nanocode_wasm.wasm` with `WebAssembly.compile`, as the Vercel adapter does.

Client text, editing and fetch helpers (2026-11-06). `nanocode-ts/client` also exports pure modules that tempoxyz/pyramid's web client carried as its own and now takes from here, each with web-platform types only and every limit passed in by the caller: `Markdown` (`complete` closes a streaming reply's unfinished tail so it renders as it will once complete: open emphasis, code spans, a link whose destination has not arrived as `Markdown.incompleteLink`, a table header without its delimiter row, and a lone `-` or `=` under a line that would read as a setext heading, touching only the last paragraph and never code, `separateLists` starts a numbered list that continues its numbering after a paragraph, which CommonMark lets interrupt a paragraph only at 1, and `blocks` splits a reply into blocks that render the same alone, so a streaming reply reparses only its last block, and keeps a reply with link definitions whole, and `fenced` says whether a text ends inside an open code fence), `Diff` (an LCS line diff after trimming the shared head and tail, which shows the middle as replaced above the caller's `maxCells`, with removals before additions as `diff -u` orders them, `collapse` for unchanged runs and `stats`), `CodeEdit` (a composer's Markdown code editing on plain text and a selection: backticks pair and wrap, a ``` line closes its fence on Enter, Enter and Tab inside a fence keep or move indentation, Backspace removes an empty backtick pair, and `codes` and `pieces` cut the text into the pieces an editor draws, with caller ranges drawn as atomic `pill` pieces), `Selection.markdown` (a selection's DOM nodes, by a structural node type, back to the Markdown they show, for Copy and Quote), `TabLeader.open` (one leader tab per browser on `navigator.locks` and a `BroadcastChannel`, with the caller's lock and channel names, a heartbeat `beat` and `heard`, the last time another tab posted, so a follower stops relying on a leader that froze holding the lock), `Retry` (which requests may be sent again, whether a failed attempt is worth sending again, jittered exponential backoff that honours a short Retry-After, Retry-After parsing and attempt deadlines that reject with a `TimeoutError`), `Reuse` (structural equality of JSON values without allocation, and `items`, which keeps each unchanged item of a fresh list and the previous list itself when nothing changed, so memoized views of it do not render again) and `Syntax.runs` (a highlighted line's tokens as the fewest runs that render the same, given the scopes a palette colors). Pyramid's measurements stay Pyramid's: its retry timeouts and backoff, its diff cell budget of 4,000,000 and its fold context. Pyramid vendors these files with `apps/web/scripts/nanocode.sh` instead of installing the release tarball, which is 43 MB with the WebAssembly modules and installs mppx, undici and the markdown renderer's unified packages for a few hundred lines of pure code. Checked with `npm run check:types`, `npm run build:ts` and `vp check` on the new files, which also pass `tsc --noUncheckedIndexedAccess --exactOptionalPropertyTypes`. Not checked: behaviour in a browser, deferred to the owner's final round.

Website feedback (2026-11-06). `nanocode-ts/feedback` and `nanocode-ts/feedback/server` let any website collect comments on its own elements and turn them into issues on its Tempo gateway app, which the app's agent can work on. The widget follows Amp's feedback widget (ampcode.com/news/close-the-loop), the handler follows tempoxyz/accounts' `Handler.auth` (a fetch handler the site mounts on its own server), and the policy callbacks follow ithacaxyz/porto's `Route.merchant({ sponsor })`.

```
page ──▶ Feedback.mount ──POST /feedback──▶ Handler.feedback (site's server, its Tempo Key)
                                              ├─ kv set issues/<id> in the app
                                              └─ turn on options.agent, if set
```

The site's server is the connection to the gateway: `Handler.feedback({ accept, admin, agent, app, fetch, gateway, identify, key, path, public, repo })` holds the key, so no key reaches a browser and the site's origin needs no proof. Its routes under `path` (`/feedback`):

| Route | Answer |
| --- | --- |
| `GET /feedback` | `{ ready: true, agent, app, wallet }` once `GET /api/keys/me` accepts the key, else 503 `{ ready: false, reason, help }` |
| `POST /feedback` | parses the report (`Issue.parse`), asks `accept({ report, request })`, stores the issue at `issues/<id>`, or `git/<repo>/issues/<n>` with `repo`, with `/v1/kv/set` (`ifVersion: null`, private unless `public`), starts a turn on `agent` with `Issue.prompt`, and answers 201 `{ id, link, agent }` |
| `GET /feedback/<id>` | `Issue.summary`: `at`, `id`, `status`, `resolution`, never the comments or the reporter |
| `GET /feedback/issues` | the issues, 100 a page with `cursor`, when `admin(request)` allows it, else 404 |

An issue is `{ version: 1, id, at, status, resolution, reporter, report }`. Its id is `iss_` with the time in base 36 and 4 random bytes, so kv lists issues in filing order. `reporter` is what `identify(request)` returns, such as the address of an accounts `Handler.auth` session. `Issue.prompt` names the kv key and asks the agent to set `status` (`accepted` or `closed`) and `resolution` when it is done, so the agent closes the issue with its own `kv_set` tool. The handler sets no limits of its own: the gateway's kv refuses a value over 64 KiB and keeps it 90 days (its retention), and the handler forwards the gateway's status and detail. A key check that fails is not cached, so a fixed key takes effect on the next request. `fetch` sends every gateway request when given, for a host that reaches the gateway through a binding: a Cloudflare Worker on the same account as the gateway calls it through a service binding, as gakonst/swarms does.

With `repo`, the board is that repository's issues, the tempoxyz/gateway convention `git/<repo>/issues/<n>` (its AGENTS.md: a repository's issues are kv values with that key, and there is no issues API). The handler takes `n` from the counter value `git/<repo>/issues`, `{ next }`, with a compare-and-swap `kv/set` (`ifVersion` the version it read, or `null` when there is none), and retries only on 409, which means another filer advanced the counter. It then writes the issue create-only, and a 409 there (a number taken by hand) takes the next number. The counter key has no trailing slash, so a list of `git/<repo>/issues/` leaves it out. An issue's id is then `n`, its link `/feedback/<n>`, and the widget reads "Filed as #n". The gateway's kv keeps a value 90 days from its last write, so a board idle for 90 days starts again at 1, and numbers whose issues remain are skipped. Checked against the mock gateway, made atomic like the gateway's single-statement writes: eight concurrent posts took 1 to 8 once each, and after a hand-written `issues/10` with the counter at 10, the next post took 11.

`Feedback.mount({ excerpt, parameter, storage, target })` waits for `DOMContentLoaded` when the page has no `body` yet, as for a script in `<head>` (gakonst/swarms loads it there, and the first version threw on `document.body.append`), and shows nothing until the page opens with `?feedback` (`parameter`) or a report from that origin was sent, which `nanocode.feedback.shown` in `storage` records until Hide. It then asks `target.status()`. If that answers `ready: false`, the panel says "Feedback is not set up" with the reason and its help link, and the launcher reopens that panel instead of picking. The launcher starts picking: a transparent overlay takes the pointer, so the page's links and buttons do not run, and `document.elementsFromPoint` without the widget's host finds the element under it. A click opens a comment form at that element. Each comment gets a numbered pin at its element, New comment picks another, and Send passes the report to `target.send`. `Report.describe` records each comment's note, CSS selector (`Report.selector`: the nearest unique id, then tags with `:nth-of-type`), tag, text cut to `excerpt` characters with `…`, and rectangle, and the report adds the page's title, its URL without `parameter` and the viewport. `Report.prompt` writes them as the numbered list an agent reads. `Endpoint.create({ help, url })` is the target for a handler (`url` `/feedback`): a 404 from `url` reads "Nothing answers at /feedback", and a 503 shows the handler's reason. `Gateway.create({ url })` sends to the gateway's own support route, `POST /v1/feedback`, for the gateway's site.

| State | Shows | Leaves on |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `closed` | the launcher | launcher: `setup` when unready, else `picking`, or `reviewing` with comments |
| `setup` | why feedback is not set up, and its help link | Close, Esc or the launcher: `closed` |
| `picking` | overlay, outline under the pointer, hint | click: `writing`; Esc or Cancel: `reviewing` or `closed` |
| `writing` | the element outlined, the comment form | Save: `reviewing`; Esc or Cancel: `reviewing` or `closed` |
| `reviewing` | the comments, New comment, Send, Hide | Send: `sending`; New comment: `picking` |
| `sending` | Sending, or a code and link when a target asks for an approval | result: `sent` or `failed`; Cancel aborts: `reviewing` |
| `sent` | the target's label and link | Done: `closed` |
| `failed` | the error | Try again: `sending`; Back: `reviewing` |

The widget renders into an open shadow root with the plain stylesheet in `Feedback.styles.ts`, not Zyzz, because a host page loads no stylesheet for it and must share no class names with it. tempoxyz/gateway serves the widget as one classic script, `/feedback.js`, which posts to `data-endpoint` (`/feedback` on the page's own origin) or, with `data-owner` and `data-app`, to the gateway's own copy of this handler at `/p/<owner>/<app>/feedback`, for sites without a backend. That copy uses `Issue` from this module and takes reports only from the origins the owner listed (its AGENTS.md describes it). `examples/feedback` is a Worker that serves a page with that script and mounts the handler (`npx wrangler dev -c examples/feedback/wrangler.jsonc`, with `TEMPO_KEY`, and `TEMPO_AGENT` and `TEMPO_ADMIN` when wanted, in `examples/feedback/.dev.vars`). Its `accept` takes only same-origin posts, and its `admin` takes a bearer secret.

Checked on 2026-11-06 with the example under wrangler 4.152.0 against a mock gateway (a Node server answering `/api/keys/me`, `/v1/kv/set`, `get` and `list` and `/v1/agents/:id/turns` as tempoxyz/gateway does) and headless Chrome, with `/feedback.js` bundled by esbuild from these sources (13 KB, 5 KB gzipped). A page with no handler and an example without a key showed the setup panel with "Nothing answers at /feedback" and "no Tempo Key" and their links, and the launcher reopened that panel. The configured page was hidden without `?feedback`. Two comments filed a private create-only issue in app `feedback-example` whose selectors were `#hero` and `body > section > div:nth-of-type(2) > button`, and the agent `site` got one turn naming `issues/<id>` and both notes. The status route read `open` and then the resolution the mock wrote as the agent, without the comments. The admin list returned the issue with the secret and 404 without it. Over curl, a missing `title` and a `javascript:` URL answered 400, a post from another origin 403, and an unknown issue 404. The first version of this widget, earlier the same day, held a Tempo Key in the page's `localStorage` and signed in through the gateway's device flow from the browser. The handler replaced it, so a browser never holds a key. On production on 2026-11-06, with a one-day Tempo Key of the test wallet, the example Worker filed a real issue in app `feedback-example` through `Handler.feedback`, and the app's agent (`codex` on DeepSeek V4.1 Flash) read it from kv and set it `accepted` with its resolution, which the status route returned.

Session state and its log are written in the rows a turn writes anyway (2026-11-04). `open` and `src/wasm/branch.rs` use the SDK's `RowStore` instead of `SqlStore`, with `SessionStore::with_logged_prompts()`, so a turn's admission is written with its next commit, and the SDK's `SessionLog` format 2 keeps a turn's events in its `log_turns` row and in `log_chunks` rows of up to 16 KiB. The first open after a deploy converts the object's `SqlStore` tables (`states`, `owners`, `records`, `state_records`) into `state_rows` and `state_pages` and the log's `turns` and `events` into `log_turns` and `log_chunks` and drops the old tables, so a Worker version from before cannot read a converted object. Events are published as they arrive and written at the next `tool.call`, steer, end of turn or `settle`, so after a crash a client may hold events the log no longer has, and seqs continue 16,384 above the highest stored one, so no client cursor skips the new events. `Checkpoints.persisted` counts `state_rows`, `state_pages` and unfinished `log_turns`. Through `output/vs-agents/rows-nc.ts` (Node, mock Responses server) rows written per turn went from 17.1 to 3.1 for a text turn, 27.3 to 8.3 for a cell with one tool call, 57.3 to 20.3 for ten parallel calls and 87.3 to 50.3 for twenty sequential ones, with turn CPU unchanged within the noise of two interleaved `bench-nc.ts` runs at load average 24 to 27, and `journal-crash.ts` killed at 1, 20, 60, 200, 360, 520 and 900 ms finished the turn with no lookup run twice. Since SDK 3f8067f80 the log keeps unfinished turns' rows in memory instead of reading each before writing it, writes events at top-level tool calls but not at a cell's nested ones, and reads its open state in the migration's transaction: `rows-nc.ts` wrote 3.1, 7.3, 19.3 and 31.3 rows per turn, and nanocodexyz/durable-vercel's drill, run with its scripted model calling `charge` through `exec`, committed 11 Workflow batches and 59 events per session with durability on, against 12 and 62 on nanocode-ts 16bec28 and 14 to 15 and 68 to 71 on 6e92b79, because the Vercel journal cut a batch at every read that followed queued writes. With reads no longer cutting batches under Durable On, the same drill committed 8 batches and 50 events for each provider in four interleaved runs at load average 54 to 62, every check passing, with recovery and the second turn as fast or faster (median 394 ms and 199 ms for Claude, against 481 and 334 ms).

The workspace shell's Claude Bash results are cut as the SDK's native foreground executor cuts them (2026-11-05): `HostShell`'s `ForegroundExecutor` in `src/wasm/workspace.rs` keeps the first `max_stdout` and `max_stderr` bytes at a character boundary and reports the cut as `truncated`. It used `redact_and_limit`, the Codex `exec_command` format that `ShellExecutor` keeps, which puts a `Warning: truncated output (original token count: N)` header, a `Total output lines` line and a `…N tokens truncated…` marker into stdout, and Code Mode scripts parse and split that stdout: in gakonst/opentempi's recorded turns of 2026-11-05 a `JSON.parse` failed on the header and a script passed the header and the `Total output lines` line to `wc -c` as file paths. With SDK 97b32dc31 a nested call keeps 128 KiB: in opentempi's local Worker a cell's `tools.Bash` of `rg -l` over 6,632 files returned 131,072 bytes of paths with `truncated: true` and no notice in stdout.

SQL hosts route each call on the SDK's access mark (2026-11-05, SDK bee3d45e7). The SDK's `Sql::run` carries `SqlAccess`: `SqlStore` and `RowStore` mark their reads `Read` and their format migrations `Schema`, and every other call is `Write`. `WorkerStore` (`src/wasm/store.rs`) sends the mark as the `access` field (`read`, `write` or `schema`) of the `store` and `log` host operations. `AgentRuntime` passes it to `SqlHost.transaction` as an optional fourth argument, so a host written before it gets every call as one transaction, as before. `Sql.create` runs a read without `transactionSync` and reports the mark in each `Observation`. `LocalSql.run` runs a read without `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` and prepares a schema statement outside its statement cache.

`Journal.create` compares `total_changes()` and the schema version around every transaction whatever its mark and journals every one that changed the database, and a journaled schema transaction keeps its mark for replay. From 27aa7f8 until 2026-11-06 it skipped that check for reads, and the SDK's `RowStore::acquire_owner` (its `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE SET owner, fence = fence + 1 ... RETURNING`) was marked `Read`, so every owner fence bump was missing from the replayed batches: nanocodexyz/durable-vercel's drill charged twice after a SIGKILL in the middle of a charge in all four provider and durability combinations, and passed with the check forced for reads (found by that repository's drill on 2026-11-06). SDK 7abcf5c68 marks the acquisition `Write`, and the journal no longer trusts a read mark: a transaction marked `read` that changed the database rejects with `Fatal` (`nanocode: the <kind> <operation> transaction is marked read but changed the database`), poisons the journal and halts the drive, so a mislabeled write fails the session by name instead of reaching the log unmarked or not at all. On 2026-11-07 nanocodexyz/durable-vercel's `npm run drill` passed all eight runs with this check over build-28336bc's module (one charge each; no transaction marked `read` changed the database), and over build-4e35cf7's module, whose SDK marks `acquire` `read`, the first drive halted with `nanocode: the store acquire transaction is marked read but changed the database` before the charge, where without the check it charged twice. Through `output/durability/restart.mjs` (below), a one-cell turn sent `acquire` three times marked `read` on 4e35cf7's module and three times marked `write` on this build. `SqlFileSystem` marks its table creation `schema` and its seven read operations (`stat`, `get`, `links`, `resolve`, `list`, `children`, `tail`) `read`. The SDK bump from 6727a5f1e changed one Rust line: `TurnInbox<WorkerStore>` is `TurnInbox<SessionLog<WorkerStore>>`. The module is 13,783,378 bytes (4219854 with gzip -9), against 13,785,215 (4,216,736) in the release asset of 0af2a69. No host here uses D1, Postgres or a lease, so nanocode-store-sql's migration gate (`after`, `allowed`) and nanocode-sync's `Lease` and `keep_lease` (native only) have no user in this package.

Agent sessions survive a restart in the middle of an operation (2026-10-30). A Durable Object reset or a deploy used to end every cell and hand operation in flight: the recovered turn reported the step as interrupted by a host crash with an unknown outcome. Cells: `open` gives `LocalCells` `CellJournals::with_sink(WorkerJournals)` (`src/wasm/journals.rs`), which writes each cell's journal ahead to `nanocode_cell_journals` through the durability `store` (the SDK's points: when the cell starts, before a nested call is dispatched, once the cell has consumed a result and has nothing left to run, when an observer begins and when a cell yields; an update with a header replaces the cell's rows; a retired cell's rows are deleted, and a cell that an `exec` or `wait` observed finish keeps them, marked finished, until the session next starts a cell), and restores the scope's journals when a runtime opens, so the recovered `exec` or `wait` replays the cell and observes it. Hand operations: the Cloudflare App logs every request it sends to a hand (`hand_requests`, keyed by the hand's name and the runtime ID of its catalog, with the request's session and call IDs and a deadline of `REMOTE_TIMEOUT_MS` plus the yield the operation requested, the same bound a live operation has) and stores each reply frame before the executor sees it. A hand that registers on a new connection with a runtime that has unanswered requests is sent one `resume` listing them (a resume makes the hand forget retained replies it does not list, so it lists all of them), and rows of the hand's other runtimes are deleted, because those can never answer. The transport `remote::host` builds (`Recovering`, over the SDK's `RemoteClient`) answers `RemoteTransport::recover` with the `remote_recover` host operation: the stored reply, or the reply once it arrives, until the request's deadline or at least `REMOTE_TIMEOUT_MS`, or nothing when the hand comes back as another runtime; `NOT_RECEIVED` and `REQUEST_IN_USE` replies come back as unsent, so the step may be dispatched again. A pump that recovers a running turn waits up to `REMOTE_TIMEOUT_MS` for the hands with unanswered requests to attach, because recovery routes by the hands attached at that moment. Replied rows are deleted `REMOTE_TIMEOUT_MS` after their reply and unanswered ones that long after their deadline. Checked through tempoxyz/gateway's local Worker, restarting it about 10 s into each Codex turn (every Durable Object reset and every socket closed; 10.6 to 11.2 s down): a cell writing a line every 5 s under a `wait` finished with its four lines once each; a cell running on an `nc hand` (0.6.7) that ran `sleep 20; echo finished-on-hand` finished with `finished-on-hand` after the hand redialed (`Wall time: 20.09 seconds`, one run); and a cell in the agent whose `exec_command` was running on a tools-only `Hand.connect` hand in Node finished with `nested-finished` (20.06 s, one run). No event of the three turns mentions a host crash or an unknown outcome. Before, the same restart ended the first and second with `Tool execution interrupted by a host crash; outcome unknown`. Since 2026-11-04 `WorkerJournals` appends each update to the cell's latest row (`body || char(10) || update`) while that row stays within one SQLite leaf page: 4,061 bytes of a 4,096-byte page (`usable size - 35`, the SQLite file format), less 70 bytes of record header and integers and the scope and session ID. So an append rewrites one page at most, and a larger update starts a row of its own. 4,096 bytes is SQLite's default page size. The page size of workerd's SQLite was not measured. The thread-local `BATCHES` map holds each cell's open row, and a header update or `forget` resets it. After a restart every adopted cell writes a header first, so the map needs no recovery. A sequential call now costs one row write, and a retired cell one or a few deletes instead of one per update. Measured with `output/vs-agents/rows-nc.ts` (Node 22.23, the agents-vs-nanocode mock, 20 turns each, SDK 40588ecad): a turn whose cell makes 20 sequential calls wrote 87.3 rows instead of 103.3, and 10 parallel calls 57.3 instead of 65.3. `output/vs-agents/journal-crash.ts` kills a child process with SIGKILL while its cell makes 6 sequential 150 ms calls, then restores the session from the same SQLite file and drives the turn. In runs killed after 200, 360, 520 and 900 ms, every call started once, the call in flight at the kill returned an unknown outcome, and the remaining calls ran. Journals the previous build wrote to `nanocode_cell_journals` restored the same way (kills at 360 and 700 ms). Until 2026-11-06 (SDK 69eb33022) a finished cell's rows were deleted as soon as its `exec` observed it finish, while the codex backend writes a lone tool call's result with the session's next commit, after the next model call, so a restart during that model call recovered the `exec` as interrupted, found no journal and answered `Tool execution interrupted by a host crash; outcome unknown` for a cell that had completed. tempoxyz/gateway's agents hit it three times in two and a half hours on 2026-11-06 (Swarms swarm f7df8db3fe54, 7 s and 16 s after the cells finished). The SDK now writes a finished mark on the journal and deletes the rows when the session next starts a cell, which comes only after the model call that asked for it and its tool step's begin were committed; a reopened runtime keeps finished journals aside, revives one only for the step that recovers its call, and deletes the rest at the next cell. `output/vs-agents/cell-done-crash.ts` (Node 22.23, node:sqlite, a mock Responses server, the child killed with SIGKILL and the session reopened from the same file; `SCENARIO=one|two|turns`, `KILL_AFTER_REQUEST`, `KILL_MS`, `CALL_MS`), on the production package (nanocode-ts 0af2a69) and then on this build: killed 1 s into the model call after `exec(lookup a)`, the recovered `exec` answered the host crash before and `lookup said {"key":"a","value":42}` after, with `lookup` run once; with two `exec`s, killed 1 s into the last model call, right after `lookup b` ended, or 2 and 10 ms into the model call that asked for `exec b`, both kept their real output (at 2 ms the model call was recovered and asked again); killed during `lookup b`, its outcome was unknown, as before; three turns with a restart before the third left only the newest finished cell's rows. Until 2026-11-06 (SDK 65707b556) a cell wrote nothing until its first nested call, a `wait` or a yield, so a restart while a cell slept, computed or made no call at all, and a restart during the model call after a finished cell that made no call, recovered the `exec` as interrupted with no journal and answered the host crash for a cell that had dispatched nothing (tempoxyz/gateway's Swarms agents hit it twice in one swarm). The SDK now saves the cell's header before the script starts; recovery replays the header-only journal from its start, which is safe because every dispatch is journaled before it runs. `output/durability/restart.mjs` (Node 22.23, node:sqlite, a mock Responses server, the child killed with SIGKILL `KILL_MS` after the model's answer and the session reopened from the same file; `SCENARIO=plain|sleep|compute|quick|yield|yieldtext`, `WASM`), on 4e35cf7's module and then on this build, killed 1 s in: a cell that sleeps 3 s and then calls `lookup`, and one that busy-loops 3 s first, answered the host crash before and `lookup said {"key":"a","value":42}` after, `lookup` run once; a cell that only prints, with the kill in the 3 s model call after it, answered the host crash before and its output after; killed during `lookup`, its outcome was unknown, as before. Killed between the tool step's commit and the header's save (up to about 40 ms after the mock's answer in a cold child) the `exec` is still a host crash. It costs one `cell_journal_save` per cell (26 to 27 SQL transactions for a one-cell turn), two for a cell without calls. In Miniflare with the Durable Object adapter (`output/durability/cf/run.mjs`, the bundle built from `dist` with nanocode-cloudflare-durable's `local-runtime.mjs`, the instance disposed and recreated on its persisted state 2.5 s into a cell that sleeps 6 s, `SCENARIO=nap`), the recovered `exec` answered the host crash on 4e35cf7 and `woke` (wall time 6.1 s) on this build. A cell journal's header stores its tool catalog once per session scope instead of once per cell (2026-11-07). The SDK puts the whole nested-tool catalog (`ALL_TOOLS` metadata with input and output schemas) in every cell's header, so a replayed cell sees the tools it ran with. In tempoxyz/gateway an agent has 124 nested tools (the gateway's MCP tools, web search, subagents, the harness tools), whose catalog is 183 KB of JSON (175 KB stored), and every cell wrote it again. `WorkerJournals::save` now replaces a header's `tools` with `[{"$nanocode_tools": <digest>}]` and writes the catalog to `nanocode_cell_tools` with `INSERT OR IGNORE` in the same transaction, unless this module instance already wrote or loaded that digest for the scope (thread-local `CATALOGS`). `load` reads the catalogs that a stored journal line names (`instr` over the batch bodies), puts them back in the headers, marks them known, and deletes the scope's catalogs that no journal names and this instance does not know, so a catalog an in-flight save relies on is never pruned. A header whose catalog is missing is dropped with its journal, as an unsaved journal is. Journals written before keep their full `tools` and restore as before. The digest only has to match within one store, so a different BLAKE3 output after a dependency change only stores another copy. Measured through tempoxyz/gateway's Worker under `vp dev` with the AI binding (payments skipped by a config outside that repository, DeepSeek V4.1 Flash, the Durable Object's `sql.exec` and `transactionSync` wrapped to log each statement's bound bytes; the turn: one cell calling `mcp__gateway__kv_set` and `kv_get` and a subagent through `spawn_agent` and `wait_agent`), build-b325614 against this change: a Codex agent's first turn bound 938 KB to SQL (700 KB of it eight header rows) and 418 KB after (journal rows 9 KB, plus the catalog once, 174 KB); its second turn 477 KB (354 KB journal) and 134 KB after (6 KB journal, no catalog); a Claude agent's first turn 1,183 KB (996 KB journal) and 348 KB after (10 KB journal plus 164 KB catalog). Transactions per turn did not change. `output/durability/restart.mjs` (`sleep`, `yield`, `compute`, `plain`: `lookup` run once and its real output after the kill), `mcp-resume.mjs` (`resume` and `lose` ran the work once, `plain` and `unknown` the unknown outcome) and `cf/run.mjs` (`progress` through six restarts completed with 19 model calls; `nap` with one restart answered `woke`) passed on this build, and `bench/hands.ts --hand=local --cells=noop,callback-seq-50 --n=5` bound 19.7 KB to SQL a `noop` turn instead of 40.9 KB (the bench's own catalog is 11 KB) with the same 19 transactions.

A nested MCP call that was in flight when the host restarted resumes when its server advertises it (2026-11-07, SDK 1e244263c). A server opts a tool in with `_meta: {"io.nanocode/resume": true}` on its `tools/list` entry; recovery of a journaled running call on such a tool calls it again with the same arguments and `x-codex-turn-metadata` (session, turn and the nested call ID the journal kept) plus `_meta["io.nanocode/resume"]: true`, and reads the answer's `_meta["io.nanocode/resume"]`: absent is the call's result, `not_seen` sends the call again as a fresh call under the same key, and `unknown` keeps the unknown outcome. A tool without the annotation is never called again, as before. Nothing in this package changed for it: the MCP tools `open` gives the runtime are the SDK's `PreparedMcpTool`, and `Mcp.defer`'s cached `tools/list` answers keep `_meta`. `output/durability/mcp-resume.mjs` (Node 22.23, node:sqlite, a mock Responses server and a mock streamable-HTTP MCP server whose tools do 4 s of work per call, the child killed with SIGKILL 1 s into the MCP call and the session reopened from the same file; `SCENARIO=resume|plain|lose|unknown`, `WASM`): with `resume`, the recovered cell answered the unknown outcome on build-28336bc and, on this build, the server got the call again with the resume flag, answered the first call's result and did the work once; `plain` (no annotation) answered the unknown outcome with no second call; `lose` (the server held the first call without recording it and answered `not_seen`) sent the call a third time without the flag and returned its result, the work run once; `unknown` answered the unknown outcome. A recovered `wait` observes its cell for the wait's own `yield_time_ms` (SDK 1f49e844e): the cell's journal keeps the observing wait's yield beside its call ID, where recovery used the yield of the `exec` that started the cell, so a long wait interrupted by a restart came back `Script running` early. `restart.mjs` with `SCENARIO=yield` (an `exec` with a 300 ms yield whose cell sleeps 3 s and calls `lookup`, then a `wait` with a 10 s yield, killed 1 s in): the recovered wait answered `Script running` after 0.3 s on build-8691526 and the cell's completed output, `lookup` run once, on this build. SDK 0a98b23a1 (2026-11-07) gives the native CLI the same recovery and makes a new exec forget journals that were restored and never adopted; in this package that only drops rows no recovery would read, since a scope's restored journals are adopted by the first exec or wait after a runtime opens. With it, `restart.mjs` `SCENARIO=sleep`, `yield` and `compute` and `mcp-resume.mjs` `SCENARIO=resume`, `plain` and `unknown` answered as above (the resumed call once with the first call's result, the plain call the unknown outcome with no second call), and the module is 12,350,030 bytes.

A Code Mode cell's `wait_agent` resumes after a restart (2026-11-07, SDK 4021bd373). Before, the subagents crate gave its tools no `Tool::recover`, so a reset while a cell waited on a subagent (tempoxyz/gateway's Swarms lead f3a42cf87339 hit it three times in 35 minutes after deploys) answered `This nested call was running when its cell moved to another host; its outcome is unknown`. A fresh wait now records, on each waited agent's subagent-journal record (`waited`: the caller's session, the nested call ID and the result's mark), which results it delivered, writes the journal before returning them, and the registry restores those marks when its scope is restored; `WaitAgent::recover` answers the results recorded under its call ID again, with their output, or waits afresh when none were recorded. `list_agents`, `interrupt_agent` and `close_agent` recover by running again (a read, and two operations whose end state is what the caller asked for); `send_agent_message` and `spawn_agent` recovered with the unknown outcome until SDK 903e5ace9 (next paragraph). `output/swarm/run.ts` (Node 22.23, node:sqlite, a scripted Responses or Messages model; `SCENARIO=wait|delivered`, `PROVIDER=codex|claude`): a lead cell spawns a child that answers after 4 s and waits on it; killed with SIGKILL 2.5 s in (`wait`), or at the cell-journal write that carries the wait's result (`delivered`, so the result had left the registry but not reached the journal), the recovered cell answered the unknown outcome on build-0b6a628's module for both harnesses, and on this build `LEAD-RESULT [[1,"completed","done-1"]]` (wait: after 4.1 s, the resumed child's answer; delivered: in 0.13 s from the record, output included). In Miniflare (`output/durability/cf/run.mjs` with `WORKER=worker-subagents.mjs SCENARIO=subwait`, the instance disposed and recreated 3 s into the wait) the recovered cell answered the unknown outcome on build-0b6a628 and the child's result after 9.9 s on this build. `restart.mjs` (plain, sleep, compute, yield), `mcp-resume.mjs` (resume, plain, lose, unknown) and `cf/run.mjs` (progress: 19 model calls; nap: woke) answered as before.

A Code Mode cell's `send_agent_message` and `spawn_agent` resume after a restart, and a cell that only sleeps resumes its remaining time (2026-11-08, SDK 903e5ace9). Before, neither subagent tool had a `Tool::recover`, so a reset while a cell sent a message or spawned a child answered `This nested call was running when its cell moved to another host; its outcome is unknown`, though the message or child was journaled, and the model might send or spawn again. Now a send records, on the recipient's subagent-journal record (`sent`: the caller's session, the nested call ID `<exec call>/code-<n>`, the message ID and, once the harness admitted it, its disposition), which message it made, and a spawn records on the child's record (`spawned`: the caller's session and the nested call ID) which call made it; their recovery answers that receipt (message, thread, sender, recipient and disposition, `queued` when the restart came before admission and the message was redelivered) or the spawn report again, and sends or spawns afresh only when the subagent journal holds no such record, which means the message or child never reached the journal and is not redelivered or resumed. A recipient keeps the records of one cell per sending session (the newest), so a session's earlier cells' records go when it sends from a new cell. The subagent journal is saved deferred, with the store's next group commit, so for a few milliseconds after a send returns its record may not be durable while the cell journal already holds the receipt; a reset in that window loses both the record and the in-memory delivery, and the recovered call sends afresh. Cells: a cell's journal entries (its `setTimeout` registrations with their wall-clock time, clock and random reads) were published, and so saved, only with an event, a nested call or a consumed result, so a cell that only slept left a header-only journal and every restart replayed its sleep from the start: under deploys every few minutes a long sleep never ended (tempoxyz/gateway's Swarms subagents, recorded by agent 53). The engine now sends `Waiting` whenever a step leaves the cell pending with unpublished entries, the host saves them, and a replay schedules each journaled timer at its original deadline (`finish_replay` already did, given the entry). A cell's journal also keeps when its observer began (`observer_since_ms`, wall clock), so a recovered `exec` or `wait` yields after what is left of its yield instead of a whole yield from the restart. `wait_agent`'s `timeout_ms` still starts again on recovery (its nested call keeps no start time); a recovered wait returns when a child finishes either way. `output/durability/sub.mjs` (Node 22.23, node:sqlite, a scripted Responses or Messages model; `SCENARIO=spawn|send`, `PROVIDER=codex|claude`, `MODE=drop`): a lead cell spawns a child, sends it `MSG-BODY-XYZ` (deferred) and waits on it; the child process drops the cell-journal save that carries the spawn's or the send's result (and later ones, so the journal ends with the call running) and is killed with SIGKILL right after the subagent journal's commit that holds the child or the message.
